● Plan read in full (856 lines, Stages 0–6). Environment checked: GNU Make
  4.4.1, Python 3.13.12 (miniconda base, no .venv yet), Docker 29.7.2.

  1. Plan in 5 bullets

  - Goal: a daily 3-day forecast (t+1..t+3 from d0, the last complete USGS day)
    of Eno River daily mean flow at USGS 02085000. Each row has a median, 80%
    and 95% bands, and the persistence baseline beside it. One
    HistGradientBoostingRegressor per horizon with default settings, predicting
    the log-change target log1p(Q[d0+h]) − log1p(Q[d0]). The bands come from
    calibration residual quantiles.
  - Data: USGS new OGC API, Open-Meteo historical-forecast (training),
    previous-runs lead-1/lead-2 rain (calibration and test rows, D20) and the
    live forecast. MRMS via IEM is used only for verification. Everything uses
    America/New_York date strings and is never converted to UTC. The splits are
    train 2018–2023, calibration 2024-02..2024-12, test 2025+.
  - Architecture: five stages (ingest, features, train, forecast, dashboard).
    They pass work to each other only through CSV/joblib/JSON files under
    DATA_DIR. Stages share only config, paths and logs, and an AST test enforces
    that. All writes are atomic. local_today() and AS_OF_DATE control every
    calendar date.
  - Safety: Forecast is the freshness gate. It refuses to run, writes nothing
    and exits 1 in these cases: USGS data more than 2 days stale, forecast
    weather that was not fetched today or has fewer than 3 days, missing flow
    lags, or a bad checkpoint. Floods are never clipped. Metrics never contain
    NaN, and small groups are marked "insufficient".
  - Delivery: the Makefile is the interface. Tests run offline (pytest-socket,
    responses, recorded real fixtures) with exactly one marker each, and CI runs
    on Python 3.12. The Streamlit dashboard shows a fan chart and at most 3
    KPIs. Docker Compose runs a scheduler and the dashboard on the named volume
    sfdata, as a non-root user, with a healthcheck.

  2. Ambiguities and suspected errors (need your call)

  Q1. The fixture date can't be 2026-09-23 any more. Today is 2026-09-24. A
  forecast response recorded now starts on 9/24, so fixture mode with
  AS_OF_DATE=2026-09-23 would have fc_fetched_date ≠ day 1 of the forecast and a
  mismatched latest d0. This breaks AC-1.7 and the smoke tests for Stages 1 and
  4.
  Recommend: record all fixtures today and use AS_OF_DATE=2026-09-24 everywhere.
  The 9/18 and 9/21 test cases stay inside the 7 past days. I'd log this in the
  Decisions log as your approved deviation. I also need your OK to make live
  HTTP calls while recording fixtures. Tests stay offline.

  Q2. AC-2.1 conflicts with the AC-2.10 availability matrix. AC-2.1 says the
  newest file wins for each date across weather_hist and weather_fc. So for the
  last ~7 days, the value comes from whichever of the two files ingest wrote
  last. The matrix says calibration and test rows use historical-forecast, but
  the live row uses weather_fc past days. The marker-value test (1.0 vs 4.0)
  will expose this.
  Recommend:
  1. Ingest writes weather_fc after weather_hist, so weather_fc wins for dates
     both files cover.
  2. The marker test picks its test row outside the 7-day weather_fc window.
  3. Implementation notes record that recent test rows use past_days values,
     which the plan already verified are identical to historical-forecast.

  Q3. AC-1.1 and its test disagree. AC-1.1 says a backfill makes five files. The
  test test_backfill_empty_dir says "four files", written before MRMS and the
  lead-matched source were added.
  Recommend: the test asserts five files with MRMS_ENABLED=1, plus a second
  check that MRMS_ENABLED=0 gives four.

  Q4. make install conflicts with AC-0.10. The plan's make install runs pip
  install -r requirements-dev.txt -e .. That installs runtime dependencies only
  if the dev file contains -r requirements.txt, and AC-0.10 requires every line
  to be an exact name==version pin.
  Recommend: make install runs pip install -r requirements.txt -r
  requirements-dev.txt -e ., and both files contain only pins.

  Q5. Where should I work? You're on master, the main branch is main, and there
  is no .venv.
  Recommend: I create .venv with Python 3.13 as the plan's install steps say. I
  don't commit unless you ask. Tell me if you want a feature branch per stage.

  Small calls I'll make myself unless you object. Each will go in the
  Implementation notes:
  - An empty USGS response exits 0 (WARNING only).
  - Pruning keeps the 5 newest files of each pattern in features/ (features_,
    rain_check_, mrms_).
  - The high-flow threshold is the 90th percentile of flow_cfs over train-split
    dates.
  - The date.today( grep scans only streamforecast/.
  - In fixture mode, ingest serves each fixture whole. Date-coverage gaps are
    logged once as a summary WARNING, not as thousands of dates.
  - forecast_<d0>_<ts>.csv names sort by d0 first. "Newest" therefore means the
    newest d0, then the newest timestamp. That is acceptable.

  Deviations (details in the plan's Stage 0 Implementation notes)

  1. make install installs both requirements files. Otherwise the dev file would
     need a -r requirements.txt line, which AC-0.10 forbids. This was Q4.
  2. Added a tzdata==2026.4 pin. Without it, zoneinfo can't find
     America/New_York on Windows or in the slim Docker image.
  3. DNS lookups are also blocked in tests. --disable-socket doesn't stop DNS,
     and a lookup happens before any socket exists, so a test could still reach
     the network. tests/conftest.py blocks it.
  4. The date.today( check scans both streamforecast/ and tests/, following your
     board doc's "grep the whole codebase".
  5. An extra test checks that config, paths and logs import no stage module and
     no HTTP or machine-learning library.

● Your venv is fine: .venv\Scripts\ contains activate, Activate.ps1 and
  activate.bat. The problem is the shell. source only exists in Bash, so you're
  almost certainly in PowerShell, which is the VS Code default terminal.

  Option A: keep PowerShell. I tested this:
  .\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
  make install
  make help
  make config
  $env:DATA_DIR='/tmp/sf-smoke'; $env:USGS_SITE='99999999'; make config
  Remove-Item Env:DATA_DIR, Env:USGS_SITE
  make lint; echo $LASTEXITCODE
  deactivate
  - In PowerShell, VAR=value make ... doesn't work, so set the variables with
    $env: first and remove them afterwards.
  - make resolves to your winget GNU make, and make config ran correctly.
  - A plus: PowerShell doesn't rewrite paths, so the override prints
    DATA_DIR=/tmp/sf-smoke exactly.

  Option B: switch to Git Bash, which is what the plan assumes. In VS Code, open
  the terminal dropdown (the ˅ next to +) and pick Git Bash. Then the plan's
  commands work exactly as written:
  source .venv/Scripts/activate
  I used this in Git Bash throughout Stage 0 and it worked.

  If activation fails in PowerShell with "running scripts is disabled", run this
  once, then activate again:
  Set-ExecutionPolicy -Scope CurrentUser RemoteSigned

  I'd go with Option B, so the smoke-test commands for every later stage paste
  unchanged.

  If activation still fails, paste the exact error and tell me which terminal
  you're in.

      352  
      353  ### Acceptance criteria
      350 -- [ ] AC-1.1 On an empty `DATA_DIR`, `make ingest` backfills U
          -SGS and historical-forecast weather from `HISTORY_START`, and 
          -previous-runs precipitation from `LEADS_START`, through yester
          -day (America/New_York), and writes one forecast file, plus MRM
          -S from `HISTORY_START` when `MRMS_ENABLED=1`. That is five new
          - files: `raw/usgs/usgs_<ts>.csv`, `raw/weather_hist/weather_hi
          -st_<ts>.csv`, `raw/weather_leads/weather_leads_<ts>.csv`, `raw
          -/weather_fc/weather_fc_<ts>.csv`, `raw/mrms/mrms_<ts>.csv`.   
      351 -- [ ] AC-1.2 On a second run, the USGS request starts at `max(
          -existing date) − USGS_LOOKBACK_DAYS`, and the historical-forec
          -ast and previous-runs requests start at `max(existing date) + 
          -1`. When nothing is new for a source, no request is made and n
          -o file is written for it. The log states each requested range.
      352 -- [ ] AC-1.3 USGS CSV columns: `date, flow_cfs, approval_statu
          -s, qualifier, last_modified`. Sorted ascending by date even wh
          -en the API returns rows unordered. `flow_cfs` is float. `quali
          -fier` is a `;`-joined string or empty. Only `statistic_id=0000
          -3` is requested.                                              
      353 -- [ ] AC-1.4 Weather CSV columns: `date, precip_mm, tmax_c, tm
          -in_c, source, fetched_at_utc`, with `source ∈ {historical_fore
          -cast, forecast}`. The forecast file holds the 7 past days plus
          - 3 forecast days. If the forecast endpoint returns fewer than 
          -3 future days (today onward) or null values, the file is still
          - written with what came back, and a WARNING `forecast returned
          - N of 3 days` is logged (Forecast decides; AC-4.7). Every requ
          -est uses `timezone=America/New_York`.                         
      354 -- [ ] AC-1.5 All HTTP goes through one function with a timeout
          -, `HTTP_RETRIES` attempts, and exponential backoff on 429/5xx/
          -connection errors. A `Retry-After` header is honoured, capped 
          -at 60 s.                                                      
      355 -- [ ] AC-1.6 On final failure (HTTP error, Open-Meteo `{"error
          -": true}` body, or malformed JSON), ingest logs an ERROR namin
          -g the source and reason, writes no partial file for that sourc
          -e, still attempts the other sources, and exits non-zero. An em
          -pty USGS response (`features: []`) is logged as WARNING, write
          -s no file, and is not a crash.

    its last 8 days.

  Findings from the real APIs

  - DST days always have 24 hours. Open-Meteo applies one fixed UTC offset to a
    whole response, the offset in force on the day you make the request. That
    holds even for January dates, whose true offset differs by an hour. The code
    treats a date as complete when all 24 hours are present. This matches how
    Open-Meteo builds its own daily totals.
  - The USGS v0 endpoint works, but its own next links point to /v1/. I kept v0
    as D1 says and follow the links exactly as given.
  - The API key goes in the X-Api-Key header, so it never appears in logged
    URLs.
  - USGS marks 2025-07-06..18 as ESTIMATED, including the 8,180 cfs Chantal
    peak. Stage 2 must keep those values (D14).
  - The fixture with an ESTIMATED day and a gap is hand-edited. No real 20-year
    window has both, so I removed one day by hand; SOURCES.md records it.

  Deviations (all 13 are in the Stage 1 Implementation notes)

  - Plan updates for your approved answers: new decision rows D27–D30 (Q1–Q4).
    The smoke-test command now uses AS_OF_DATE=2026-09-24.
  - logs.py: log lines now write to whatever stderr is current, so tests can
    capture them. The line format is unchanged.
  - Two of my own new tests were wrong, and I fixed the tests:
    - The qualifier test assumed 07-10 had no qualifier; ESTIMATED actually
      starts on 07-06.
    - The fixture-mode test read fetched_at_utc from the USGS file, which by
      AC-1.3 has no such column.
  - Added a per-year mrms: fetched N rows log line so the log matches the smoke
    test's "Watch for".

  Manual Smoke Test (PowerShell version)

  .\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
  make ingest
  Get-ChildItem data\raw\usgs, data\raw\weather_hist, data\raw\weather_leads,
  data\raw\weather_fc, data\raw\mrms
  Get-Content data\raw\usgs\*.csv -Tail 3
  Select-String -Path data\raw\mrms\*.csv -Pattern '^2025-07-0[5-7]|^2026-09-18'
  Select-String -Path data\raw\weather_leads\*.csv -Pattern '^2026-09-18'
  Get-Content data\raw\weather_fc\*.csv
  make ingest
  Get-ChildItem data\raw\usgs, data\raw\weather_hist, data\raw\weather_leads,
  data\raw\weather_fc, data\raw\mrms
  $env:INGEST_SOURCE='fixtures'; $env:AS_OF_DATE='2026-09-24';
  $env:DATA_DIR="$env:TEMP\sf-fixture"; make ingest
  Get-ChildItem -Recurse $env:DATA_DIR -File
  Remove-Item Env:INGEST_SOURCE, Env:AS_OF_DATE, Env:DATA_DIR
  The last line matters. If INGEST_SOURCE or AS_OF_DATE stays set in that
  terminal, later runs quietly use fixtures or a frozen date.

  What to expect:
  - First run (takes about 25 s):
    - usgs: fetched ~3,186 rows 2018-01-01..<yesterday>.
    - mrms: fetched ... once for each year from 2018 on.

      2026-09-09, 2026-09-10. That is a real gap in the USGS record, not a

      367    - Any MRMS failure is logged as an ERROR for that source onl
           y: the other sources are still written, and **it does not chan
           ge ingest's exit status**. Verification data must never block 
           forecasting.
      368    - `MRMS_ENABLED=0` skips it entirely.
      369  - [x] AC-1.10 **Timezone pinning.** Every Open-Meteo request (
           forecast, historical-forecast, previous-runs) includes `timezo
           ne=America/New_York`, taken from `TIMEZONE`. Ingest checks tha
           t the response's `timezone` field equals the requested value; 
           on a mismatch it logs an ERROR `timezone mismatch: requested A
           merica/New_York, got <tz>` and writes no file for that source.
            USGS `time` and Open-Meteo `daily.time` are written verbatim 
           as `YYYY-MM-DD` strings: never parsed as timestamps, never loc
           alized, never converted to UTC.

          -ested with `timezone=America/New_York`. `precip_mm` is the sum

      487     - Open-Meteo applies one fixed UTC offset to a whole respon
           se: the zone's offset on the day of the request. It returns **
           24 hourly values for every local date**, DST days included. Ve
           rified on 2025-03-09 and 2025-11-02; a January 2025 request ma
           de today also comes back at -14400. So the "23 or 25 hours on 
           DST days" in AC-1.9 can't occur.
      488     - The implementation treats a date as complete when all 24 
           of its hours are present and non-null. That matches how Open-M
           eteo builds its own daily sums, so lead and lead-0 rain share 
           day boundaries. `test_leads_dst_day_has_24_open_meteo_hours` p
           ins this down with the real DST fixtures.
      489     - **Side effect for the Tester (RK13):** day boundaries fol
           low the offset in force when the data was requested. The 2026-
           09-24 backfill cuts every day at 04:00 UTC, including winter d
           ays whose true local midnight is 05:00 UTC, and an incremental
            fetch made in winter will cut at 05:00 UTC. That is at most a
            1-hour shift, similar to the USGS standard-time ambiguity the
            plan already accepts.

  Design choices (all in the Stage 2 Implementation notes)

  - Rows only on days with an observed flow. This makes "fill gaps of at most 2
    days" safe: any gap before d0 has already ended by d0, so its length is
    known at issue time. The leakage test checks this by changing and deleting
    future flow.
  - The latest row is always kept, even with missing lags, so Forecast can
    refuse it later (AC-4.9).
  - The latest row's forecast rain comes only from the newest forecast file. A
    value missing there stays missing; it is never copied from an older file.
  - AC-1.12, Features half, now built: a WARNING when the weather grid cell
    differs between files. I had ticked AC-1.12 in Stage 1 without it; it now
    has a test.
  - Shared test setup: every test now starts with all config variables cleared,
    so leftovers in your shell can't leak into tests.
  - My own test mistakes, fixed:
    - the expected lead-2 value for 09-19 is really 1.2 mm;
    - the sensitivity check moved to a date where the 30-day rain sum exists;
    - one hand-calculated value in the gap test (40, not 41).

  AC-2.5 needs your decision

  The plan's check reads: "the first day with ≥ 25 mm of rain is the same date
  as the first day flow rises more than 10×". On the real data, 2025-07-02 had
  35.7 mm (MRMS 39.6) and flow rose 2.8× that same day, so the first ≥ 25 mm day
  is 07-02, not 07-06. That is also a same-day response, just smaller.

  The test instead checks: on the first day flow jumps more than 10× (07-06),
  rain is ≥ 25 mm, and it is the wettest day in the window.
  - ET data passes: 54.7 mm, the window maximum.
  - The GMT data fails, as it should: 7.9 mm on the jump day, with the heaviest
    rain on 07-07.

  Every exact value AC-2.5 lists is still asserted as written.

> explain more about AC-2.5

● What AC-2.5 is for

  AC-2.5 guards against one bug: rain and river flow landing on different
  calendar days.

  USGS reports each day's flow on the local (New York) calendar. Open-Meteo adds
  up rain over whatever timezone you request. If the two ever disagree, for
  example because the weather is requested in UTC, heavy evening rain gets
  counted on the next day. The model would then see the river rise the day
  before the rain and learn a false pattern: "flow rises, then it rains."

  So AC-2.5 runs a real storm, Tropical Storm Chantal in July 2025, through
  Features and checks that the big rain and the big flow jump fall on the same
  date ("lag 0"). As a control, it also feeds in the same storm requested in GMT
  and requires the check to fail, which proves the check can detect
  misalignment.

  Why the plan's wording fails on the real data

  The plan says: "The first day with ≥ 25 mm of rain is the same date as the
  first day flow rises more than 10× over the previous day." Here is the
  recorded window:

  ┌───────┬────────────┬─────────────┬──────────────┬──────────┬───────────┐
  │ Date  │ Rain, ET   │ Rain, GMT   │ MRMS radar   │  Flow    │   Flow    │
  │       │    (mm)    │    (mm)     │     (mm)     │  (cfs)   │  change   │
  ├───────┼────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
  │ 07-01 │ 0.0        │ 0.0         │ 2.5          │ 13.2     │           │
  ├───────┼────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
  │ 07-02 │ 35.7       │ 35.7        │ 39.6         │ 37.6     │ 2.8×      │
  ├───────┼────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
  │ 07-03 │ 0.0        │ 0.0         │ 0.0          │ 53.6     │           │
  ├───────┼────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
  │ 07-05 │ 0.0        │ 0.0         │ 0.0          │ 15.5     │           │
  ├───────┼────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
  │ 07-06 │ 54.7       │ 7.9         │ 178.1        │ 1,990    │ 128×      │
  ├───────┼────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
  │ 07-07 │ 15.1       │ 61.9        │ 2.0          │ 8,180    │ peak      │
  ├───────┼────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
  │ 07-09 │ 49.7       │ 0.0         │ 39.4         │ 332      │           │
  ├───────┼────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
  │ 07-10 │ 9.4        │ 59.1        │ 8.9          │ 922      │ 2.8×      │
  └───────┴────────────┴─────────────┴──────────────┴──────────┴───────────┘

  Four days before Chantal, on 07-02, a thunderstorm dropped 35.7 mm, and the
  radar (MRMS) confirms it. The soil was dry, so the river rose only 2.8×, not
  10×. So:
  - the first day with ≥ 25 mm is 07-02;
  - the first day flow rises more than 10× is 07-06.

  Those dates differ, so the check as written fails, even though the alignment
  is correct. Both storms show rain and rise on the same day. The architect
  likely wrote the rule looking only at Chantal and missed the smaller storm in
  the same window.

  What I test instead

  Start from the flow jump and look at the rain on that day:
  1. Find the first day flow rises more than 10×: 07-06.
  2. That day must have ≥ 25 mm of rain: 54.7 ✓
  3. That day must be the wettest day in the window: 54.7 is the maximum ✓

  GMT control: the jump is still on 07-06, but that day has only 7.9 mm, and the
  wettest day moves to 07-07 (61.9 mm). Both conditions fail, which is the
  result the control needs.

  Radar cross-check (unchanged): MRMS's wettest day is also 07-06 (178 mm), the
  same as the jump day and Open-Meteo's wettest day.

  I also kept every exact number AC-2.5 lists: 15.5 cfs on 07-05, 54.7 mm and
  1,990 cfs on 07-06, 8,180 cfs on 07-07, and 49.7 mm on 07-09 followed by a
  rise to 922 on 07-10.

  Is this weaker than the plan's version?

  No. It tests the same claim, "the rain that caused the jump is recorded on the
  jump day", from the flow side instead of the rain side. That is sturdier,
  because a rain day that doesn't move the river (dry soil) can't trip it up.

  The GMT control still separates correct from broken alignment, so the check
  keeps its purpose. A version so loose that GMT also passed would be a real
  weakening. This one isn't.

  Other options, if you prefer

  - A. My version (recommended): jump day has ≥ 25 mm and is the wettest day.
  - B. Keep the plan's wording, but start the search on 07-04, after the 07-02
    storm has passed. This is simpler, but choosing a start date after seeing
    the data is fitting the test to the answer.
  - C. Keep the plan's wording and record a new fixture window without the 07-02
    storm (start 07-04). Same concern as B, and it changes the fixture the plan
    names.

          - test row has `weather_lead_matched = True`; training aborts w

  Findings for the Tester (reported, not tuned):
  - Flood days are badly under-covered: the 80% band holds only 14–25% of them.
    This is the risk RK6 predicted, because the calibration year has few floods.
  - Rainy windows are under-covered too, at 39–54%.
  - Skill is higher on dry windows than on wet ones, the opposite of what the
    smoke test's "Watch for" expected. Dry recessions are smooth, so the model
    beats "no change" easily there; on rainy days the rain-forecast error
    dominates (RK3).
  - I changed nothing after seeing these numbers. Per D23, what to do about them
    is your call, after the Tester's review.

  Design points (all in the Stage 3 Implementation notes)

  - The saved model holds only scikit-learn models and plain Python values, so
    loading it in Forecast never pulls in train.py. A test checks this.
  - The metrics file has fixed key names that the dashboard will read, for
    example horizons["1"]["test"]["skill_mae"].
  - "d0+h" is worked out with real calendar dates, because the features table
    skips days with no flow reading.
  - The synthetic check (AC-3.6) uses its own 4-year calibration and test
    periods. With the default 335-day calibration year, random sampling error
    alone would push the 95% band close to its ±0.03 tolerance. Results:
    calibration 0.800 / 0.949 on every horizon, test 0.79–0.82 / 0.94–0.96,
    skill 0.43–0.49.
  - The coverage gate uses unrounded numbers, and a missing value counts as a
    fail.
  - Too little data is a clean failure: nothing is saved, it exits 1, and
    Forecast will then report "no checkpoint yet". This matters for fixture
    mode, whose features hold only 8 rows.
  - Two of my own tests were wrong and are fixed: a rain-label expectation
    (01-04's window has data, so it's "dry"), and a boundary check that now uses
    exact values.

    from the same ingest.

    and metrics_<ts>.json.
  - The JSON has horizons "1", "2" and "3", each with a test block (skill,
    coverages, n_high, by_rain) and a calibration block.
  - make coverage prints the table above, then PASS: every horizon within
    tolerance, then exit=0. The calibration columns should be about 0.80 and
    0.95 (that is the wiring check). Test coverage is the honest number.

> What is your assessment of the model performance. Does it need to be imrpoved
  (more data?) before the next stage and justify. Keep explaination high level

● No. I would not improve the model before Stage 4, and more data wouldn't fix
  the real weakness anyway.

  What's good

  - It clearly beats "no change". Forecast errors are 32–35% smaller than
    persistence at every horizon. That is real skill, not noise.
  - The bands are honest overall. On the held-out 2025–26 data, the 80% band
    holds about 80–84% of outcomes and the 95% band about 96%. Both are inside
    the tolerances you agreed to (D19).
  - The mechanics are verified. Calibration coverage lands exactly on nominal,
    the synthetic test recovers known coverage, and there is no data leakage.

  What's weak

  - Most of the skill comes from dry weather. Between storms the river falls
    smoothly, and the model predicts that recession well. "No change" can't.
  - On rainy days the skill is small, 0.09 at t+1, and the bands are far too
    narrow. Only 39–54% of wet-day outcomes fall inside the 80% band.
  - On flood days it's worse still: only 14–25% fall inside the 80% band.

  That matters, because floods and storms are exactly when paddlers and
  emergency planners care most. Right now the uncertainty bands can't be trusted
  on the days they matter most.

  Would more data help?

  Not much, and it isn't available now anyway.
  - Earlier history can't be added. The training weather source is empty before
    2018 (RK17), and forecasts made 1–2 days ahead are only archived from 2024.
  - The main cause is the rain forecast, not the model or the amount of data.
    Open-Meteo's rain forecast is often wrong 1–2 days out (the rain check
    showed many misses and false alarms against radar). Adding more years of the
    same imperfect forecasts wouldn't fix that.
  - The flood bands are narrow because the calibration year (2024) had very few
    floods. That improves slowly as new flood seasons are recorded. The data
    can't be sped up.

  Why continue to Stage 4 now

  1. The rules say so. The model passes both gates you set: skill > 0 (AC-3.8)
     and coverage (AC-3.9). D23's rule allows trying an alternative only if
     skill fails, and it didn't. The flood weakness is a documented risk (RK3,
     RK6) to report, not a bug to tune away.
  2. Tuning now would bias the test score. Adjusting after seeing the 2025–26
     results would make them look better than they really are. The plan
     deliberately forbids that.
  3. The rest of the pipeline doesn't depend on model quality. Forecast, the
     dashboard and the containers are built around the checkpoint format, which
     is stable. Any later model change drops in without rework.
  4. The right time to decide is after the Tester's review (Gate 3). You'll have
     an independent verdict then and can choose what to do with full
     information.

  What I'd suggest for later

  These are options to discuss at Gate 3, not things to do now:
  - Show the limitation on the dashboard, for example "bands are unreliable
    during storms", as RK6 already says the metrics caption should.
  - Consider bands that depend on the weather: separate uncertainty for rainy
    and dry forecast windows, calibrated on each. This targets the actual
    failure. It would be a method change, so it needs your decision and a
    Decisions-log entry.
  - Recalibrate yearly as floods accumulate, for example rolling 2025 into
    calibration once there's a newer test period.

  Bottom line: the model is good enough to build the rest of the system on, and
  it's honest about typical days. Its storm-day uncertainty is overconfident.
  That should be disclosed now and addressed after the Tester's review, not
  tuned into it before.

● All three are confirmed, both in the code and in the files your make train
  produced (model_20260925T003936Z and its matching metrics file).

  1. Splits are by date, not shuffled.

  ┌─────────┬─────────────────────┬────────────────────┬────────────────────┐
  │ Horizon │        Train        │    Calibration     │        Test        │
  ├─────────┼─────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ t+1     │ 2018-01-03 →        │ 2024-02-01 →       │ 2025-01-01 →       │
  │         │ 2023-12-30 (2,188)  │ 2024-12-30 (334)   │ 2026-09-20 (625)   │
  ├─────────┼─────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ t+2     │ → 2023-12-29        │ → 2024-12-29 (333) │ → 2026-09-20 (624) │
  │         │ (2,187)             │                    │                    │
  ├─────────┼─────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ t+3     │ → 2023-12-28        │ → 2024-12-28 (332) │ → 2026-09-20 (624) │
  │         │ (2,186)             │                    │                    │
  └─────────┴─────────────────────┴────────────────────┴────────────────────┘

  - Each split ends before the next begins, with no overlap and January 2024
    unused. All three are in date order.
  - The end dates move back one day per horizon because the date being forecast
    (d0+h) must also fall inside the split.
  - train.py contains no shuffle, sample, permutation, train_test_split or
    KFold.
  - Tests also enforce this: test_splits_time_ordered_no_overlap,
    test_edge_rows_excluded_when_target_leaves_the_split,
    test_fit_uses_train_rows_only.

  2. The metrics file reports skill against persistence for each horizon.

  ┌─────────┬───────────────────┬─────────────────────────┬───────┐
  │ Horizon │ Model error (MAE) │ Persistence error (MAE) │ Skill │
  ├─────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────┤
  │ t+1     │ 33.89 cfs         │ 49.57 cfs               │ 0.316 │
  ├─────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────┤
  │ t+2     │ 41.88 cfs         │ 64.47 cfs               │ 0.350 │
  ├─────────┼───────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼───────┤
  │ t+3     │ 43.64 cfs         │ 67.35 cfs               │ 0.352 │
  └─────────┴───────────────────┴─────────────────────────┴───────┘

  Both are scored on exactly the same test rows (n = 625 / 624 / 624). The same
  comparison is also reported for flood days and for wet and dry windows.

  3. Residual quantiles are stored with the checkpoint.
  model_20260925T003936Z.joblib holds residual_quantiles, and its model_version
  matches the metrics file.

  ┌─────────┬────────┬────────┬────────┬───────┬───────┐
  │ Horizon │  2.5%  │  10%   │  50%   │  90%  │ 97.5% │
  ├─────────┼────────┼────────┼────────┼───────┼───────┤
  │ t+1     │ −0.940 │ −0.368 │ 0.005  │ 0.321 │ 1.335 │
  ├─────────┼────────┼────────┼────────┼───────┼───────┤
  │ t+2     │ −1.233 │ −0.598 │ −0.006 │ 0.725 │ 1.680 │
  ├─────────┼────────┼────────┼────────┼───────┼───────┤
  │ t+3     │ −1.442 │ −0.724 │ −0.087 │ 0.550 │ 2.344 │
  └─────────┴────────┴────────┴────────┴───────┴───────┘

  - Each row is in increasing order, and they come from calibration rows only
    (test_quantiles_from_calibration_and_ordered,
    test_quantiles_ignore_test_rows).
  - The metrics file carries a copy, so the numbers can be inspected without
    loading the model.
  - These are errors in log-change space, which is why the bands are
    multiplicative (D9). For example, the t+1 95% band runs from about 0.4× to
    3.8× the forecast. The upper tail is wider than the lower one because the
    river can rise much faster than it falls.

● Stage 4 plan:
  - What forecast.py does: loads the newest published checkpoint (a model file
    with its matching metrics file) and the newest features row. It runs the
    refusal checks in order: no checkpoint, then stale USGS data, then missing
    flow lags, then stale forecast weather, then fewer than 3 forecast days. If
    all pass, it writes 3 rows atomically.
  - Bands: Forecast may not import train.py, so the band formula is written
    again in forecast.py. A regression test checks the two give identical
    bounds.

  What was built

  - Order of checks, stopping at the first failure:
    a. No checkpoint yet: skip, exit 0.
    b. Unusable checkpoint: refuse.
    c. USGS data more than 2 days old: refuse. At 1–2 days old it forecasts
       anyway, with a warning.
    d. Missing flow history: refuse.
    e. Weather forecast not fetched today: refuse.
    f. Fewer than 3 forecast days: refuse.

    Any refusal exits 1 and never writes or deletes a file.
  - Band formula: Forecast may not import the training code, so it has its own
    copy. A test proves the two give identical numbers on the real served
    forecast.
  - Provisional or estimated readings are flagged in the output but used as-is.

  Deviations (all 6 are in the Stage 4 Implementation notes)

  1. Smoke test commands changed. They now run make ingest and make features
     before make forecast. Forecast refuses weather that wasn't fetched today
     (AC-4.10), and your last ingest was a day or more ago, so the plan's
     original sequence would refuse. Only the commands changed.
  2. The 80% band doesn't always widen with horizon. The 95% band widens t+1 →
     t+3 as expected. The 80% band is wider at t+2 than at t+3, because the
     calibration errors happen to be larger at t+2 (90th percentile 0.725 vs
     0.550). That comes from the data, not a bug, so the smoke test's "bands
     widen" holds only for the 95% band. It's a note for the Tester, related to
     the small calibration set (RK6).

    refusal added nothing.
  - Forecast never writes 1- or 2-row files. With fewer than 3 forecast days it
    refuses outright (test_short_forecast_refuses,
    test_null_forecast_value_refuses).

  2. lo95 ≤ lo80 ≤ median ≤ hi80 ≤ hi95 on every row. This is guaranteed three
  ways:
  - The math: every bound applies the same increasing transform to the
    calibration quantiles, which are already in order.
  - A guard before writing: if the order were ever broken, Forecast would refuse
    instead of writing.
  - Tests: test_band_ordering_and_nonnegative covers 5 cases, including 0 cfs,
    0.22 cfs and 8,180 cfs.

  3. Bounds never negative.
  - The smallest value in your file is 0.62 cfs.
  - Every bound is computed as max(0, …). Even absurd negative calibration
    errors give exactly 0, never below
    (test_large_negative_residuals_clip_at_zero).

  4. It skips cleanly when there's no checkpoint. A run on a brand-new empty
  folder:
  WARNING forecast no checkpoint yet; skipping forecast
  exit=0
  It wrote no files. A model file without its matching metrics file also counts
  as "no checkpoint yet" (test_checkpoint_without_sidecar_is_unpublished).

  Decisions and deviations (all 7 are in the Stage 5 Implementation notes)

  - Test network rule, the risk the plan flagged in Stage 0:
    - AppTest needs a local socket pair on Windows, which the offline test mode
      blocks.
    - I allowed loopback (127.0.0.1) only, for the dashboard tests only. Every
      other host stays blocked, and a test proves an outside connection still
      fails.
  - The dashboard reads the metrics file of the model that made the displayed
    forecast, not the newest one. If that file is missing, 2 tiles show
    "unavailable" with a caption naming the model, and it still shows 3 tiles.
  - The flood caption is my reading of the plan's RK6 note that the dashboard
    should state the storm limitation.
  - Chart details:
    - The forecast fan starts at the latest observation, so it joins the
      observed line.
    - Forecast days already past are grey and labelled "past".
    - The y-axis is linear, with no log toggle and no other controls.
  - The headline rounds to 2 significant digits and names the weekday of the t+3
    forecast.

    - A chart with 60 days of observed flow running into a widening fan: dark
      80% band, light 95% band, and a dashed orange "no change" line.

    traceback.

    the plan's trigger for adding a log-scale toggle.
  - Stop: Ctrl+C in both terminals. Close Terminal 2 or run Remove-Item
    Env:DATA_DIR, Env:DASHBOARD_PORT so the settings don't linger.

  present.

● Fixed: the forecast line itself now turns grey on past days, matching the
  legend.

  What was wrong:
  - Only the forecast points were coloured by status. The median line was a
    single blue line across every day, so on a past day you'd see a grey dot
    sitting on a blue line.
  - The legend always listed "past", even when no forecast day was past.

  What changed (in dashboard.py (streamforecast/dashboard.py)):
  - The median line is split into two segments. The grey past segment runs from
    the last observation through the last past day, and the blue forecast
    segment carries on from there. They join, so the line has no gap.
  - The legend lists only what is on the chart. When nothing is past, there's no
    grey "past" entry.

  Tests: I added two unit tests for the split and the legend. The app tests now
  also check that the median line layer is coloured by status. The old chart
  fails those checks, so this bug can't come back unnoticed.

  once the forecast goes stale. You can preview that by setting

● The flow axis is now log-scale, and the rain panel is on top of the chart.
  There was never a grey line to show, because nothing in your forecast was in
  the past yet.

  The grey line: your forecast was issued from data through 09-23 and covers
  09-24 to 09-26. Your clock was on 09-24, so no forecast day had passed, and
  the legend correctly listed only "forecast". Grey only appears once the
  forecast goes stale. The caption no longer mentions "grey" unless a grey
  segment is actually drawn. To preview the stale case, run the dashboard with a
  later date:
  $env:AS_OF_DATE='2026-09-25'; make dashboard
  You should see a grey line from the last observation to 09-24, and a "Forecast
  outdated" banner. Afterwards run Remove-Item Env:AS_OF_DATE so the date
  doesn't stick.

  The changes (in dashboard.py (streamforecast/dashboard.py)):
  - Log flow axis: the axis is labelled "cfs (log scale)", and every flow layer
    uses it.
    - A log axis can't show 0, and a lower 95% bound can be exactly 0 at very
      low flow. Values below 0.1 cfs are drawn at 0.1, but tooltips still show
      the true numbers. The lowest flow in the record is 0.22 cfs, so real flows
      are never affected.
  - Rain panel on top: it shares the date axis with the flow chart.
    - Blue bars are Open-Meteo rain for the past 60 days.
    - Orange bars are the forecast rain the model used for the next 3 days.
    - It's the same rain the model sees, not radar rain, and it all comes from
      the features file, so the dashboard still reads only its allowed files.

  This replaces the plan's "linear axis by default", so I logged it as decision
  D33 in your name and added it to the Stage 5 notes.

      242  - [x] AC-0.8 `paths.local_today()` returns `AS_OF_DATE` when t
           hat is set, otherwise `datetime.now(ZoneInfo(TIMEZONE)).date()
           `. With time frozen at 2026-09-23 23:30 America/New_York (03:3
           0 UTC on 9/24) and `AS_OF_DATE` unset, it returns 2026-09-23. 
           With `AS_OF_DATE=2026-09-23` it returns 2026-09-23 on any real
            date. No module calls `date.today()` (grep in the test). Cale
           ndar-date logic (today, yesterday, staleness, fetch dates) goe
           s through `local_today()`. Real UTC timestamps for log lines, 
           file names, and `created_at_utc` use `paths.utc_now()` and are
            not affected by `AS_OF_DATE`.
      243  - [x] AC-0.9 **Atomic writes everywhere.** `paths.atomic_write
           (path, write_fn)` writes to `<path>.tmp` in the same directory
            and then calls `os.replace`. Every stage uses it for every pu
           blished file (raw CSVs, features, rain check, MRMS verificatio
           n file, checkpoint, sidecar, forecast). A glob for any publish
           ed pattern never matches a `.tmp` file.

  Build summary (Stages 0–6)

  A daily 3-day forecast for the Eno River, with 80% and 95% uncertainty bands
  and a "no change" baseline beside it:
  - Stage 1: downloads data from 5 sources.
  - Stage 2: builds a leak-free feature table.
  - Stage 3: trains one model per forecast day.
  - Stage 4: writes the forecast, or refuses if the inputs are stale.
  - Stage 5: a dashboard with rain drawn upside down above a log-scale flow
    chart.
  - Stage 6: runs it all in two containers on a daily schedule.

  On real data the model beats "no change" by 32–35% and meets its coverage
  targets. Its bands are too narrow on storm days, and the dashboard says so.

  Deviations

  Your approved decisions (logged in the plan):

  ┌─────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ ID  │                              Decision                              │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D27 │ Fixture date is 09-24 instead of 09-23.                            │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D28 │ Forecast-file weather wins on a timestamp tie.                     │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D29 │ The backfill test expects 5 files with MRMS on, 4 with it off.     │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D30 │ make install installs both requirements files.                     │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D31 │ A previous-runs day is complete when all 24 Open-Meteo hours are   │
  │     │ present.                                                           │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D32 │ The Chantal same-day check is tested from the flow jump.           │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ D33 │ Log flow axis, rain hanging upside down, no gridlines.             │
  └─────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  My own interpretations (explained in each stage's notes):
  - Features rows exist only on days with a flow reading.
  - The latest row is always kept.
  - AC-3.6's synthetic check uses its own 4-year calibration and test periods.
  - The dashboard tests may use loopback only.
  - The Stage 4 and Stage 6 smoke-test commands were adjusted so they run as
    written.
  - New in Stage 6: the dashboard's host port is now ${DASHBOARD_PORT:-8501}.
    The default is still 8501, but AC-6.3 literally says 8501:8501, so this
    needs your approval (question 1).

  What the Tester should look at closely

  - Storm-day uncertainty: the 80% band covers only 14–25% of flood days and
    39–54% of wet windows. That was reported, not tuned (D23).
  - Reproducibility: only direct dependencies are pinned; their sub-dependencies
    can drift.
  - Day boundaries: Open-Meteo applies the timezone offset in force on the day
    of the request, so days shift by an hour between summer and winter
    downloads.
  - Checks I replaced or relaxed: D31, D32, and the custom synthetic splits.
  - The loopback-only socket exception for the dashboard tests.
  - Fixtures: 5 are hand-edited, and each edit is logged in SOURCES.md.
  - Band widths: the 80% band is wider at t+2 than at t+3 on the real model.
  - "Forecast outdated" is normal: Forecast refuses unless the data was
    downloaded that same day.
  - AC-0.6: no real GitHub Actions run yet, because nothing has been pushed.

  Manual Smoke Test for Stage 6 (PowerShell)

  Before starting:
  1. Stop your local dashboard (Ctrl+C in its terminal). It still holds port
     8501, and Docker can't use that port while it runs.
  2. Open a new terminal, and check that no settings from earlier smoke tests
     are left over. This should print nothing:
  Get-ChildItem
  Env:AS_OF_DATE,Env:INGEST_SOURCE,Env:MAX_STALE_DAYS,Env:DASHBOARD_PORT
  -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue

  Terminal 1:
  .\.venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
  make pipeline; "exit=$LASTEXITCODE"
  docker compose build
  docker compose up
  Terminal 2:
  docker compose ps
  docker compose logs pipeline --tail 30
  docker compose exec dashboard id -u
  docker compose exec dashboard ls -l /data/raw/usgs /data/features /data/models
  /data/forecasts
  Open http://localhost:8501. Then, once the first forecast has appeared:
  docker compose down
  docker compose up -d
  docker compose exec dashboard ls -l /data/forecasts
  docker compose run --rm pipeline make test
  docker compose down
  What to expect:
  - make pipeline: a pass summary, then exit=0.
  - Pipeline logs: download → features → train → forecast, each ending with its
    "wrote …" line, then sleeping 24h, about 40 s after start.
  - docker compose ps: dashboard (healthy) within about a minute.
  - id -u: 1000.
  - Browser: the hydrograph.
  - After down / up -d: the same forecast file is still listed.
  - make test in the container: 366 passed.
  - docker compose down: returns in a couple of seconds.

  Gate 2 comes next: please run this smoke test before starting the Tester
  conversation. If you're saving transcripts, export this Builder conversation
  now.
